## Verdict: RETURN for a one-sentence Planner amendment (N1)

All eleven RETURN findings are resolved, and I checked each resolution against the code (table below). In particular:

- **H1:** per-window currentness never freezes a Site, and the requeue recovery works with the built `enqueue` and `_LIVE_JOBS`.
- **H2:** the tick-only walkthrough can be reached, and its superseded no-op is deterministic.
- **M2:** the definition of an active Site includes KOBO-001.
- **Rule 8:** the amendment is clearly marked as pending Architect ratification, not assumed.

One new defect came in with the H1 fix. Packet :20 says to run S4 match "**every tick**" for every selected Site. However, `PostgresFindingStore.apply` inserts an append-only `finding_match_pass` row on every pass, including zero-write passes. This is not idempotent: the insert has a fresh `uuid4()`, there is no uniqueness constraint, and migration 0009's `ledger_append_only` trigger forbids deleting rows. At the one-minute cadence that is about 1,440 permanent rows per Site per day for a Site where nothing changed.

This contradicts the packet's own rule at :22: "do not add unbounded tick rows by default". The implementer cannot follow both instructions. The fix is one sentence (N1), and it can go into the same commit as the Architect's storage ratification. A targeted check of that sentence is enough; this does not need a full re-review.

### H1 trace (against the code)

- **No Site freeze.**
  - `match_state` (`postgres_finding_store.py:400-415`) sets `matched` to latest pass `selection_seq >= window max(seq)`. The pass seq is the Site-wide `max(seq)` (`_fence`).
  - A matcher that runs after each S3 stage therefore keeps every window `matched`, whether or not one window has a FAILED job.
  - `finding_episode.py:12-14` leaves only episodes that touch a non-CURRENT window unchanged.
  - The currentness gate ("no intersecting live S3 job" over the window plus its six-hour bracket) blocks only windows that intersect the failed job. Other windows advance, which is what phase 1 now proves.
- **Requeue is real.**
  - `enqueue` (`postgres_assessment_store.py:255-274`) widens only a `PENDING` overlapping job. With only a FAILED job present it INSERTs a new PENDING job, which `$assessInserts` already allows.
  - The worker processes it with `cohort=None`, which gives selection `DEFAULT` (`assessment_worker.py:115`).
  - `_select` always INSERTs a selection with `selected_at = now`, even when an identical run is reused (`:343-353`). That is later than the FAILED job's `available_at` (its failure instant, `:240-242`).
  - So `_LIVE_JOBS` (`:88-91`) stops counting the failure.
  - `assess` (EXPLICIT) would not do this, and the packet says so.
  - Carry N5: the requeue window must overlap the failed job's window.

### H2 trace (against the walkthrough and recompute)

The released late reading is the withheld GOOD mean itself (`s402c_late_power_walkthrough.py:14-21`). Acceptance writes the 0012 `EVIDENCE_ACCEPTED` work and an S3 job. The pre-tick read is then STALE/`TECHNICAL_INPUT_CHANGED` with the work ID.

The first tick runs these stages in a fixed order:

1. S3 assesses the window including the late ID.
2. S4 appends r2, and the 0011 trigger enqueues r2's work.
3. The drain no-ops r1's work as `NO_OP_SUPERSEDED = "FINDING_REVISION_SUPERSEDED"` (`financials/recompute.py:63`) and prices r2.

The order within a tick is fixed, so the result does not depend on the order in which work is drained. "Drive first delivery and all subsequent steps by full schedule ticks" removes the current step-1 and Leg B CLI calls (`:222`, `:292-296`), and a command-log grep records W4. Carry N6 covers the grep's coverage and the bounded-stage edge.

## New finding

### N1 (Medium, acceptance gap: contradictory instruction; RETURN item): "S4 match every tick" appends an unbounded append-only `finding_match_pass` row per Site per minute

- **Where:** packet :20 ("run S3, then S4 match **every tick**, even with due, leased or FAILED S3 work") against :22 ("do not add unbounded tick rows by default").
- **Code:**
  - `postgres_finding_store.py:132-137` INSERTs `finding_match_pass (id = uuid4(), ...)` on every `apply`, including zero-write passes (Q18).
  - Migration 0009 :157-174 has no uniqueness on `(site, selection_seq, matcher_version)`, and its `ledger_append_only` trigger forbids UPDATE or DELETE.
  - At a one-minute tick, an unchanged Site therefore gains about 1,440 permanent rows per day. Every pass also replans the whole Site, the matcher scan named in the risks.
  - Nothing in the packet bounds this, and the Finding writer is INSERT-only under rule 8, so the rows can never be pruned.
- **Why it matters:** an implementer who follows :20 literally builds permanent, unbounded growth in an evidence-lineage table that `match_state` and the S5 seam read. An implementer who follows :22 instead has to invent a skip rule under pressure, and a naive skip ("skip when `matched`") could miss a status-only change that the matcher must observe.
- **Fix (Planner, one sentence at :20):** run S4 for a selected Site when there is no pass for the current `MATCHER_VERSION`, or when the Site's matcher fence (`_fence`: selection seq, job count/marker, revision count) differs from the fence recorded in `schedule_site_head` at its last completed pass.
  - Otherwise record "S4 skipped: unchanged fence" in the mutable head, and append no `finding_match_pass` row.
  - This keeps the H1 property: a FAILED window never blocks matching when other windows get new selections, because any new selection moves the fence.
  - Add a phase-1 proof: N idle ticks append zero `finding_match_pass` rows, and a new selection triggers exactly one pass.
  - Because it touches `schedule_site_head` content, the Architect's storage ratification can carry it.

## Implementer carries (assuming N1 is applied; no further Planner round)

- **N2 (Low): how the timeouts reach the existing stores.** The S3, Finding and consequence adapters each build their own engine. Apply `statement_timeout`/`lock_timeout` per stage child, for example through libpq `options=-c statement_timeout=...` on the child's `DATABASE_URL`, `PGOPTIONS`, or role-level settings on the schedule credential. Do not edit the other writers' engine construction. `SET` is not matched by rule 8's `$sqlWrite`, but any new SQL or driver import outside the eight writers still fails the guard. The orchestrator and the S5 seam must reach the database only through writer or store modules.
- **N3 (Low): not every `FindingFenceMoved` is retryable.** `_insert_revision` also raises `FindingFenceMoved("a cited evidence ID is not accepted in this Site")` (`postgres_finding_store.py:173`), which is deterministic. Report it separately, by message or as a distinct type in the writer, or alarm on repeated `FENCE_MOVED` for one Site. Otherwise a permanent matcher defect retries forever and looks transient.
- **N4 (Low): define "registered evidence" in the active-Site predicate.** It should mean at least one `accepted_evidence` row, or explicitly also a registered gateway/source. Test a newly imported Site with neither, which must not be selected unless enrolled, and one with a gateway but no evidence.
- **N5 (Low): `assess_dispatch requeue` must overlap the failed job's window.** Superseding a failure is overlap-based in `_LIVE_JOBS`. Prefer `--job-id`, or validate that `--start/--end` covers the FAILED job's window. A requeue elsewhere would leave the failure live and be easy to misread as recovered. Keep the CLI's tenant handling by UUID. It must not inherit the consequence CLI's `demo`-only refusal, and nor may the stage children: the consequence stage calls the worker by IDs, never `recompute_consequences run`.
- **N6 (Low): walkthrough grep and the bounded-stage edge.**
  - Extend the W4 grep to `recompute_consequences run` as well as `process-jobs`/`match-findings`.
  - Configure per-tick limits so the first post-release tick completes S3 and S4 for KOBO-001.
  - If the S3 stage times out in that tick, the drain would instead produce the S4-02c same-revision state. The tool must then report the actual outcome, not assert either.
- **N7 (Info):** carries 1-4 of the RETURN review still apply: real IDs only, skipped distinguished from zero-work, the CLI kept, and the new rule 8 guard comment quoted in the implementation packet.

## Residual risk not already named

- `lease_job`'s expire-to-FAILED UPDATE becomes Site-scoped together with leasing. A Site that drops out of the predicate, with no live work, keeps an expired last-attempt LEASED job LEASED until it is selected again. The "remains selected while live S3 work exists" rule covers this only if LEASED counts as live work in the predicate, which it does under `_LIVE_JOBS`. The implementer should test it.
- Ratification is pending. If the Architect refuses or narrows the eighth writer, the packet's phase 1 storage text needs a matching edit before implementation. The Status line already says so.

## USER_REVIEW_REQUIRED

False still holds: operator CLI and report, backend read seam and runbook only. S5-01 keeps the owner checkpoint.
# Architect answers: S4-02d schedule storage and guard (2026-10-14)

**Ruling: ratify with the boundaries below.** The plan fix round 1 proposes one durable owner of environment and Site scheduling state. Migration 0013 may add `schedule_tick_head`, `schedule_site_head`, `schedule_cursor`, and `schedule_enrolment`. `backend/assetops_backend/scheduling/adapters/postgres_schedule_store.py` is the eighth PostgreSQL writer and the sole runtime writer of those four tables. This state is neither an accepted-evidence record, an S3 analytic result, a Finding, nor a financial consequence. Putting claims, heartbeat, enrolment, and fairness cursor into an existing domain adapter would give that adapter unrelated write authority and obscure restart and cross-host behavior. Site inventory may be read through the Site port; Site-scoped S3 leasing and job updates stay in the existing assessment writer. Consequence claims, attempts, and revisions stay in the consequence writer and its existing triggers.

## Rule 8 amendment allowed

Amend `tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1` for **that exact schedule adapter path** and **those four tables only**. Add the adapter to the PostgreSQL writer allowlist and the non-vacuity checks (an observed SQL write and driver import). Permit `INSERT` to the four schedule tables and `UPDATE` of their mutable schedule state: environment tick claim/lease, start/success/failure, heartbeat and retry due in `schedule_tick_head`; Site claim/lease, stage, selected/processed/deferred counts and last failure in `schedule_site_head`; fair position in `schedule_cursor`; explicit enrolment state in `schedule_enrolment`. The implementation should name the actual allowed columns in the guard, then test that unlisted columns and tables fail. Claims and deadlines use database time. The migration owns DDL; it is not an extra runtime writer.

Keep the guard's reverse ownership checks: no other runtime module may write any of these four tables, and the schedule adapter may write no other product table. In particular, it may not write `recompute_job`, `analytic_run_selection`, Finding tables, consequence revisions/attempts/work, accepted evidence, Foundation Site rows, or financial assumptions. Do not use a wildcard SQL or driver exception, permit `DELETE`/`TRUNCATE`/runtime DDL, or weaken the existing seven writer checks. If a schedule domain composition imports this adapter, register that domain in the guard's adapter-isolation list as well; callers receive its port, not the adapter. A migration trigger, if proposed later, requires separate review rather than silently widening this ruling.

## Installation and residual ownership

**Installation gate confirmed.** Do not install or enable a persistent schedule on any owner-visible host, including the demo host, before `I-late-reading-series-and-money-limit` lands. An automatic tick would otherwise advance the known run-1 late-reading case to an apparently current, too-low amount on the existing S4-02b money panel. Isolated walkthrough ticks and private local development are allowed for proof. The real-Site pilot still has its separate pilot gates. When the environment timer is installed, retire the older per-Site consequence timer as the packet requires.

- **S4-02c I3, clock skew:** S4-02d owns DB-clock heartbeat, claim expiry, retry due, STOPPED detection, and the S5 read seam. It must also supply DB time to the existing consequence runner's `now` input for scheduled drains, so an app clock cannot shift its retry timing. Keep the existing consequence writer as owner of consequence rows; the schedule writer gets no write access there. Prove skewed application clocks against DB time in phase 2. A one-off operator invocation should use the same DB-clock default or explicitly report an override. Do not close I3 on a heartbeat-only fix.
- **S4-02c I4, concurrent overlapping ingestion chunks:** S4-02d's independent review owns a two-chunk overlap/race proof while a tick is active: no deadlock left unresolved, no falsely CURRENT selected window, and eventual reprocessing after a moved basis. The schedule uses live job, selection-sequence and match-pass comparisons; its claim must not be described as serialising ingestion. If that proof finds an ingestion transaction or lock-order defect, carry the fix to the evidence/ingestion owner through a separate scoped packet and keep I4 open until verified. Do not mark I4 resolved merely because the schedule runs.

No owner decision is needed for these storage and guard boundaries or residual assignments. They preserve the reviewed product contract and add no owner-facing copy or visual state. The owner should receive the plain summary below; an owner checkpoint is required if implementation adds owner-facing UI/copy, and S5-01 retains its planned owner visual checkpoint. A fresh independent plan/implementation review remains required by the packet.

Rule 8 may admit only this module's inserts and named schedule-state updates on those tables, with reverse ownership, adapter isolation and existing seven-writer protections retained. S3 jobs, Findings, evidence and financial records remain under their current writers. The environment timer may be tested privately but must not be installed on an owner-visible host until `I-late-reading-series-and-money-limit` lands; installation replaces the per-Site consequence timer. S4-02d owns DB-clock resolution of I3 for scheduled work and an independent concurrent-ingestion proof for I4; any ingestion defect belongs to a separately scoped evidence fix. No owner decision is required unless owner-facing behavior changes. ## Outcome and authority

An environment-owned, bounded tick runs S3 `process-jobs`, then S4 `match-findings` for every active tenant/Site, then the existing S4-02c consequence drain. A late accepted reading reaches a revised FindingRevision and consequence without an operator invoking the two Leg B commands. Operators can see per-Site backlog, last success/failure, retry and stopped-worker conditions. S5-01 cannot mark a selected window CURRENT while relevant upstream work or an unobserved/stopped worker could leave it behind.

Authority: `.agent/architect-S4-02c-answers.md` R1, `D-2026-10-14-s402c-late-sequence-and-s5-money`, `D-2026-10-14-s5-selected-window-overview`. This packet owns registry node `I-analytic-finding-worker-schedule` (S). The separate F1 node owns run-1 sequence numbering and money limitation semantics; this packet does not change S3 cohort rules, Finding limits or price eligibility.

Built anchors: `.ai/CODE_STATE.md` S3-01/S4-01/S4-02c; `backend/assetops_backend/analytics/assess_dispatch.py` (`process-jobs`, `match-findings --tenant-id --site-id`); `analytics/assessment_worker.py` (reconcile, leased jobs and retry); `backend/assetops_backend/recompute_consequences.py` and `financials/recompute.py` (Site claim, attempt-derived retry); `backend/README-db.md` (one-minute per-Site consequence-timer runbook); `tools/s402c_late_power_walkthrough.py` (Leg A/B proof). The current `process-jobs` CLI reconciles all assessed Sites and drains up to 1000 globally leased jobs; `match-findings` is a one-shot Site matcher; the consequence CLI serves only the configured development tenant and one named Site. Do not claim those CLIs already cover all active production Sites.

## Schedule, state and S5 contract

1. **Owned Site inventory and cadence.** One timer per environment invokes a server-side tick, initially every minute. An active Site is a persisted, tenant-scoped imported Site with registered evidence or explicit schedule enrolment, whose lifecycle is neither `DECOMMISSIONED` nor `ARCHIVED`; `PLANNED` qualifies (including KOBO-001). A Site with live S3 or pending consequence work remains selected until that work is resolved, even if its enrolment changes. Report excluded Sites that still have work; never silently discard it. Enumerate by stable IDs from the persisted Site registry on every tick; â€œnewly activatedâ€ in proof means newly imported/enrolled, not a lifecycle transition. Bound Sites, Site-scoped S3 leases, and consequence items per tick with a durable fair cursor. Extend the assessment store's currently global `lease_job` to filter by Site inside the existing assessment writer; its global FIFO cannot provide Site fairness. Run `reconcile_all()` each tick and reconcile every selected Site, including never-assessed Sites; measure its global unbounded cost, without silently omitting it. For each selected Site run S3, then S4 match **every tick**, even with due, leased or FAILED S3 work. The matcher's CURRENT-window rule leaves those windows' episodes unchanged; unrelated current windows can advance. Drain consequence work after Finding commits. A bounded tick may leave work for later ticks.
2. **Concurrency and recovery.** Use database claims for the environment tick and Site stage sequence across hosts; local timer overlap prevention is insufficient. Never overlap S3 leasing/processing and S4 matching on the same Site, including a second host. The matcher fence includes Site recompute-job statuses; report `FindingFenceMoved` as retryable typed S4 `FENCE_MOVED`, not a generic success. Preserve S3's five-minute lease and retry and S4-02c's Site claim/append idempotency. An expired/crashed claim resumes without duplicate output. Bound tick and Site wall time with server-side `statement_timeout` and `lock_timeout`, plus a killable child process per stage for a Python hang. The Site S3 stage bound must be shorter than the five-minute lease; after a killed job, expose its lease expiry and retry, never assert immediate completion. Both DB stall and nonreturning-stage injections must yield typed `TIMEOUT` and retryable work. Give schedule-level discovery/stage failures retry/backoff without advancing success; do not add an independent retry to S3 or consequence rows. A terminal FAILED S3 job stays live and will not be re-enqueued by `reconcile`; expose its ID and `last_error`, alarm, and provide `assess_dispatch requeue --tenant-id --site-id --start --end` through the existing `AssessmentStore.enqueue` path for an operator to re-enqueue that default window after fixing the cause. Document the command and confirm the new DEFAULT selection supersedes the old failure; `assess` creates an EXPLICIT run and is not recovery.
3. **Durable state and health.** Migration `0013` adds `schedule_tick_head` (mutable environment owner, last tick start/success/failure, heartbeat, retry due and claim age), `schedule_site_head` (mutable per-Site claim/stage, selected/processed/deferred counts and last failure), `schedule_cursor` (mutable fair position), and `schedule_enrolment` (mutable explicit Site enrolment). Use DB claims or equivalent expiring ownership in these records; do not add unbounded tick rows by default. A new `backend/assetops_backend/scheduling/adapters/postgres_schedule_store.py` is the sole writer for these tables. Amend `tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1` rule 8 to name this eighth writer, its exact tables and allowed updates, with a reviewed S4-02d guard comment; this guard change is in scope and awaits Architect ratification, not pre-approved. Keep durable heads bounded; if attempt history is added, set a retention policy while preserving the latest failure and aggregate counts. Derive S3 backlog from live jobs, S4 backlog from existing `PostgresFindingStore.match_state` / `finding_match_pass` versus `analytic_run_selection.seq`, and consequence backlog from `ConsequenceStore.pending_work` with attempt count/age. Also count pre-0012 latest consequence heads whose read says `STALE` plus `NO_WORK_RECORDED` as distinct `STALE_NO_WORK` entries with Finding/revision IDs. This slice reports those discrepancies and blocks CURRENT; it does not backfill them. A read-only operator report gives each Site and aggregate `RUNNING`, `BACKLOG`, `RETRY_DUE`, `FAILED`, `STOPPED` or healthy idle. Only a completed zero-work tick advances heartbeat; claimed/skipped, failed and timed-out ticks do not. Repeated Site-claim skips, stale in-flight claims, old failures and growing uncapped consequence attempts trigger alarms and documented recovery.
4. **S5-01 consumption.** Add a backend Python scoped read/port taking `(tenant_id, site_id, [window_start, window_end))`. Return the S3 `UNASSESSED|PENDING|CURRENT|STALE_PROJECTION|FAILED` read status and limits, accepted input/source basis, relevant live S3 job IDs/status/errors, selected `analytic_run_selection.seq` and run/revision IDs, `match_state` pass ID/matched-through seq and matched Finding revision IDs, consequence head/work IDs, status/attempts/age (including `STALE_NO_WORK`), schedule state/heartbeat, DB `clock_timestamp()`, and a derived upstream eligibility status with stage/reason. The accepted-source watermark is the selected S3 input basis plus its `analytic_run_selection.seq`; do not use disposition timestamps or a later heartbeat as a commit-order fence (`D-2026-10-11-s3-phase-2-selection-and-read`). Acceptance transactionally creates/widens a job. For each window and its six-hour continuity bracket, CURRENT eligibility requires no intersecting live S3 job, current S3 basis, `match_state.matched = true`, no relevant outstanding or stale-without-work consequence, and a healthy observed schedule; a stopped/unobserved schedule blocks it even with empty queues. Use DB time for heartbeat/STOPPED threshold and avoid app-clock assumptions. First build with work is `PENDING`; an old result with advanced basis/work is `STALE_PROJECTION`; failed with no usable prior result is `FAILED`, preserving typed stage/error. The existing S3 read status, matcher pass and pending-work derivations are authority; S5-01 consumes this seam and applies its separate F1 money rule and same-basis `CURRENT/PRICED` test. Historical IDs remain readable.

**Per-environment ownership and timer transition (phase 2).** On installing this tick, retire the S4-02c one-timer-per-Site consequence schedule in `backend/README-db.md`; keep its CLI for one-off operator recovery. A simultaneous legacy timer would hold the Site claim and produce false skip alarms. Document exact identity, credential, cadence, bounds, stop threshold and restart commands in that runbook.

| Environment | Tick owner and identity | Cadence / gate |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Local development | Developer's local scheduler; local database credential | Optional one-minute tick on a private DB; remove the old per-Site timer when enabled. |
| Demo host | Deployment operator; dedicated schedule credential scoped to that database | One minute only after `I-late-reading-series-and-money-limit` lands; before then do not install. |
| Isolated walkthrough database | Walkthrough runner with isolated DB credential | Explicit tick invocations for repeatable proof; no persistent timer. |
| Real-Site pilot | Pilot deployment operator and separately provisioned schedule credential | Not installed until `real_pilot_requires` and other pilot gates close; then one environment timer at reviewed cadence. |

## Risks and carries

- S3's existing global `reconcile_all()` can be expensive as Sites grow; measure a multi-Site tick and keep bounded scheduling truthful. Do not omit reconcile to meet a timer budget.
- The matcher fence aggregates all Site recompute-job statuses; its `string_agg` can grow without bound. Track it alongside global reconcile cost. Serialise S3 and S4 for a Site even across hosts.
- S4 matching currently scans a Site's selected reads. Large Sites may require a reviewed bounded cursor or dirty-window selection to meet the tick limit; do not mark a partially matched Site caught up.
- S4-02c retries have no terminal cap: a deterministic failed row can append about 90 attempts/day. Keep the growing count and backlog observable and alertable; do not hide it by reporting only due work.
- A hung consequence runner can hold a Site claim while later ticks report skipped. Treat repeated skips/claim age as a stopped-progress alarm, with operator recovery; do not count skipped as success.
- Backdated configuration/model changes can dirty assessed windows without a fresh evidence job. The S3 reconcile and S4 basis comparison must detect them before CURRENT is possible.
- Do not install the schedule on an owner-visible host before `I-late-reading-series-and-money-limit` lands: it would automatically move late evidence to the run-1 too-low consequence on the S4-02b money panel.
- S4-02c I3/I4 remain open: clock skew now affects heartbeat/STOPPED if application time is used, and concurrent ingestion chunks can move a basis while a tick runs. Use database time and live-job/selection/match comparisons; carry I4 to the independent review rather than treating this schedule as its resolution.

Registry comparison: `queue_baseline_nodes` already contains `I-analytic-finding-worker-schedule`; keep it there and map its `task` to S4-02d. S5-01's `I-site-overview-read` remains gated on this node and the separate F1 node. No baseline node moves.

Out of scope: F1 late-reading sequence semantics (`I-late-reading-series-and-money-limit`), new cohort or fuel model, new owner UI/copy, S5-01 implementation, real gateway source-time attestation, and a general estate scheduler for unrelated domains.

Run `reconcile_all()` each tick and reconcile every selected Site, including never-assessed Sites; measure its global unbounded cost, without silently omitting it. For each selected Site run S3, then S4 match **every tick**, even with due, leased or FAILED S3 work. The matcher's CURRENT-window rule leaves those windows' episodes unchanged; unrelated current windows can advance. Drain consequence work after Finding commits. A bounded tick may leave work for later ticks.
+1. **Owned Site inventory and cadence.** One timer per environment invokes a server-side tick, initially every minute. An active Site is a persisted, tenant-scoped imported Site with registered evidence or explicit schedule enrolment, whose lifecycle is neither `DECOMMISSIONED` nor `ARCHIVED`; `PLANNED` qualifies (including KOBO-001). A Site with live S3 or pending consequence work remains selected until that work is resolved, even if its enrolment changes. Report excluded Sites that still have work; never silently discard it. Enumerate by stable IDs from the persisted Site registry on every tick; “newly activated” in proof means newly imported/enrolled, not a lifecycle transition. Bound Sites, Site-scoped S3 leases, and consequence items per tick with a durable fair cursor. Run `reconcile_all()` each tick and reconcile every selected Site, including never-assessed Sites; measure its global unbounded cost, without silently omitting it. For each selected Site run S3, then run S4 only when there is no `finding_match_pass` for the current `MATCHER_VERSION`, or the Site's matcher fence (`_fence`: selection seq, job count/marker, revision count) differs from the fence stored in `schedule_site_head` at the last completed pass. Otherwise record `S4 skipped: unchanged fence` in that mutable head and append no pass row. Due, leased or FAILED S3 work does not by itself suppress a match when the fence moves. The matcher's CURRENT-window rule leaves those windows' episodes unchanged; unrelated current windows can advance after a new selection moves the fence. Drain consequence work after Finding commits. A bounded tick may leave work for later ticks.
 2. **Concurrency and recovery.** Use database claims for the environment tick and Site stage sequence across hosts; local timer overlap prevention is insufficient. Never overlap S3 leasing/processing and S4 matching on the same Site, including a second host. The matcher fence includes Site recompute-job statuses; report `FindingFenceMoved` as retryable typed S4 `FENCE_MOVED`, not a generic success. Preserve S3's five-minute lease and retry and S4-02c's Site claim/append idempotency. An expired/crashed claim resumes without duplicate output. Bound tick and Site wall time with server-side `statement_timeout` and `lock_timeout`, plus a killable child process per stage for a Python hang. The Site S3 stage bound must be shorter than the five-minute lease; after a killed job, expose its lease expiry and retry, never assert immediate completion. Both DB stall and nonreturning-stage injections must yield typed `TIMEOUT` and retryable work. Give schedule-level discovery/stage failures retry/backoff without advancing success; do not add an independent retry to S3 or consequence rows. A terminal FAILED S3 job stays live and will not be re-enqueued by `reconcile`; expose its ID and `last_error`, alarm, and provide `assess_dispatch requeue --tenant-id --site-id --start --end` through the existing `AssessmentStore.enqueue` path for an operator to re-enqueue that default window after fixing the cause. Document the command and confirm the new DEFAULT selection supersedes the old failure; `assess` creates an EXPLICIT run and is not recovery.
-3. **Durable state and health.** Migration `0013` adds `schedule_tick_head` (mutable environment owner, last tick start/success/failure, heartbeat, retry due and claim age), `schedule_site_head` (mutable per-Site claim/stage, selected/processed/deferred counts and last failure), `schedule_cursor` (mutable fair position), and `schedule_enrolment` (mutable explicit Site enrolment). Use DB claims or equivalent expiring ownership in these records; do not add unbounded tick rows by default. This slice reports those discrepancies and blocks CURRENT; it does not backfill them. A read-only operator report gives each Site and aggregate `RUNNING`, `BACKLOG`, `RETRY_DUE`, `FAILED`, `STOPPED` or healthy idle. Only a completed zero-work tick advances heartbeat; claimed/skipped, failed and timed-out ticks do not.  4. **S5-01 consumption.** Add a backend Python scoped read/port taking `(tenant_id, site_id, [window_start, window_end))`. The accepted-source watermark is the selected S3 input basis plus its `analytic_run_selection.seq`; do not use disposition timestamps or a later heartbeat as a commit-order fence (`D-2026-10-11-s3-phase-2-selection-and-read`). Acceptance transactionally creates/widens a job. For each window and its six-hour continuity bracket, CURRENT eligibility requires no intersecting live S3 job, current S3 basis, `match_state.matched = true`, no relevant outstanding or stale-without-work consequence, and a healthy observed schedule; a stopped/unobserved schedule blocks it even with empty queues. Use DB time for heartbeat/STOPPED threshold and avoid app-clock assumptions. First build with work is `PENDING`; an old result with advanced basis/work is `STALE_PROJECTION`; failed with no usable prior result is `FAILED`, preserving typed stage/error. The existing S3 read status, matcher pass and pending-work derivations are authority; S5-01 consumes this seam and applies its separate F1 money rule and same-basis `CURRENT/PRICED` test. Historical IDs remain readable.

S5-01 cannot mark a selected window CURRENT while relevant upstream work or an unobserved/stopped worker could leave it behind.

 ## Schedule, state and S5 contract
 
-1. **Owned Site inventory and cadence.** One timer per environment invokes a server-side tick, initially every minute. An active Site is a persisted, tenant-scoped imported Site with registered evidence or explicit schedule enrolment, whose lifecycle is neither `DECOMMISSIONED` nor `ARCHIVED`; `PLANNED` qualifies (including KOBO-001). A Site with live S3 or pending consequence work remains selected until that work is resolved, even if its enrolment changes. Report excluded Sites that still have work; never silently discard it. Enumerate by stable IDs from the persisted Site registry on every tick; “newly activated” in proof means newly imported/enrolled, not a lifecycle transition. Bound Sites, Site-scoped S3 leases, and consequence items per tick with a durable fair cursor. Extend the assessment store's currently global `lease_job` to filter by Site inside the existing assessment writer; its global FIFO cannot provide Site fairness. Run `reconcile_all()` each tick and reconcile every selected Site, including never-assessed Sites; measure its global unbounded cost, without silently omitting it. For each selected Site run S3, then S4 match **every tick**, even with due, leased or FAILED S3 work. The matcher's CURRENT-window rule leaves those windows' episodes unchanged; unrelated current windows can advance. Drain consequence work after Finding commits. A bounded tick may leave work for later ticks.

For each selected Site run S3, then run S4 only when there is no `finding_match_pass` for the current `MATCHER_VERSION`, or the Site's matcher fence (`_fence`: selection seq, job count/marker, revision count) differs from the fence stored in `schedule_site_head` at the last completed pass. Otherwise record `S4 skipped: unchanged fence` in that mutable head and append no pass row. Due, leased or FAILED S3 work does not by itself suppress a match when the fence moves. The matcher's CURRENT-window rule leaves those windows' episodes unchanged; unrelated current windows can advance after a new selection moves the fence. Drain consequence work after Finding commits. A bounded tick may leave work for later ticks.
 2. **Concurrency and recovery.** Use database claims for the environment tick and Site stage sequence across hosts; local timer overlap prevention is insufficient. Never overlap S3 leasing/processing and S4 matching on the same Site, including a second host. The matcher fence includes Site recompute-job statuses; report `FindingFenceMoved` as retryable typed S4 `FENCE_MOVED`, not a generic success. Preserve S3's five-minute lease and retry and S4-02c's Site claim/append idempotency. An expired/crashed claim resumes without duplicate output. Bound tick and Site wall time with server-side `statement_timeout` and `lock_timeout`, plus a killable child process per stage for a Python hang. The Site S3 stage bound must be shorter than the five-minute lease; after a killed job, expose its lease expiry and retry, never assert immediate completion. Both DB stall and nonreturning-stage injections must yield typed `TIMEOUT` and retryable work. Give schedule-level discovery/stage failures retry/backoff without advancing success; do not add an independent retry to S3 or consequence rows. A terminal FAILED S3 job stays live and will not be re-enqueued by `reconcile`; expose its ID and `last_error`, alarm, and provide `assess_dispatch requeue --tenant-id --site-id --start --end` through the existing `AssessmentStore.enqueue` path for an operator to re-enqueue that default window after fixing the cause. Document the command and confirm the new DEFAULT selection supersedes the old failure; `assess` creates an EXPLICIT run and is not recovery.
-3. **Durable state and health.** Migration `0013` adds `schedule_tick_head` (mutable environment owner, last tick start/success/failure, heartbeat, retry due and claim age), `schedule_site_head` (mutable per-Site claim/stage, selected/processed/deferred counts and last failure), `schedule_cursor` (mutable fair position), and `schedule_enrolment` (mutable explicit Site enrolment). Use DB claims or equivalent expiring ownership in these records; do not add unbounded tick rows by default. Amend `tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1` rule 8 to name this eighth writer, its exact tables and allowed updates, with a reviewed S4-02d guard comment; this guard change is in scope and awaits Architect ratification, not pre-approved. Keep durable heads bounded; if attempt history is added, set a retention policy while preserving the latest failure and aggregate counts. Derive S3 backlog from live jobs, S4 backlog from existing `PostgresFindingStore.match_state` / `finding_match_pass` versus `analytic_run_selection.seq`, and consequence backlog from `ConsequenceStore.pending_work` with attempt count/age. Also count pre-0012 latest consequence heads whose read says `STALE` plus `NO_WORK_RECORDED` as distinct `STALE_NO_WORK` entries with Finding/revision IDs. This slice reports those discrepancies and blocks CURRENT; it does not backfill them. A read-only operator report gives each Site and aggregate `RUNNING`, `BACKLOG`, `RETRY_DUE`, `FAILED`, `STOPPED` or healthy idle. Only a completed zero-work tick advances heartbeat; claimed/skipped, failed and timed-out ticks do not. Repeated Site-claim skips, stale in-flight claims, old failures and growing uncapped consequence attempts trigger alarms and documented recovery.

+### Implementer carries from fresh re-review
+
+- **N2 — timeout propagation.** S3, Finding and consequence stores each construct an engine. +- **N3 — deterministic matcher failure.** `FindingFenceMoved` can also mean a cited evidence ID is not accepted in this Site. Distinguish that deterministic defect from a moved fence, or alarm on repeated `FENCE_MOVED` for the same Site so it cannot retry invisibly forever.
+- **N4 — registered evidence.** Define the active-Site predicate's “registered evidence” as at least one `accepted_evidence` row, or explicitly include registered gateway/source state. Prove an imported Site with neither is excluded unless enrolled and a Site with a gateway but no evidence follows the chosen definition.
+- **N5 — failed-job requeue.** `assess_dispatch requeue` must overlap the FAILED job's window for the new DEFAULT selection to supersede that failure; prefer `--job-id` or validate `--start/--end`. Preserve UUID tenant handling, avoid a demo-only restriction, and invoke the scheduled consequence stage by IDs rather than its one-off CLI.
+- **N6 — tick-only walkthrough.** Grep the full command log for `recompute_consequences run` as well as `process-jobs` and `match-findings`.  Built anchors: `.ai/CODE_STATE.md` S3-01/S4-01/S4-02c; `backend/assetops_backend/analytics/assess_dispatch.py` (`process-jobs`, `match-findings --tenant-id --site-id`); `analytics/assessment_worker.py` (reconcile, leased jobs and retry); `backend/assetops_backend/recompute_consequences.py` and `financials/recompute.py` (Site claim, attempt-derived retry); `backend/README-db.md` (one-minute per-Site consequence-timer runbook); `tools/s402c_late_power_walkthrough.py` (Leg A/B proof). The current `process-jobs` CLI reconciles all assessed Sites and drains up to 1000 globally leased jobs; `match-findings` is a one-shot Site matcher; the consequence CLI serves only the configured development tenant and one named Site. Do not claim those CLIs already cover all active production Sites.
 
 ## Schedule, state and S5 contract
 
-1. **Owned Site inventory and cadence.** One timer per environment invokes a server-side tick, initially every minute. An active Site is a persisted, tenant-scoped imported Site with registered evidence or explicit schedule enrolment, whose lifecycle is neither `DECOMMISSIONED` nor `ARCHIVED`; `PLANNED` qualifies (including KOBO-001). A Site with live S3 or pending consequence work remains selected until that work is resolved, even if its enrolment changes. Report excluded Sites that still have work; never silently discard it. Enumerate by stable IDs from the persisted Site registry on every tick; “newly activated” in proof means newly imported/enrolled, not a lifecycle transition. Bound Sites, Site-scoped S3 leases, and consequence items per tick with a durable fair cursor. Extend the assessment store's currently global `lease_job` to filter by Site inside the existing assessment writer; its global FIFO cannot provide Site fairness. Run `reconcile_all()` each tick and reconcile every selected Site, including never-assessed Sites; measure its global unbounded cost, without silently omitting it. For each selected Site run S3, then S4 match **every tick**, even with due, leased or FAILED S3 work. The matcher's CURRENT-window rule leaves those windows' episodes unchanged; unrelated current windows can advance. Drain consequence work after Finding commits. A bounded tick may leave work for later ticks.
+1. **Owned Site inventory and cadence.** One timer per environment invokes a server-side tick, initially every minute. Run `reconcile_all()` each tick and reconcile every selected Site, including never-assessed Sites; measure its global unbounded cost, without silently omitting it. For each selected Site run S3, then run S4 only when there is no `finding_match_pass` for the current `MATCHER_VERSION`, or the Site's matcher fence (`_fence`: selection seq, job count/marker, revision count) differs from the fence stored in `schedule_site_head` at the last completed pass. Otherwise record `S4 skipped: unchanged fence` in that mutable head and append no pass row. Due, leased or FAILED S3 work does not by itself suppress a match when the fence moves. The matcher's CURRENT-window rule leaves those windows' episodes unchanged; unrelated current windows can advance after a new selection moves the fence. Drain consequence work after Finding commits. A bounded tick may leave work for later ticks.
 2. **Concurrency and recovery.** Use database claims for the environment tick and Site stage sequence across hosts; local timer overlap prevention is insufficient. Never overlap S3 leasing/processing and S4 matching on the same Site, including a second host. The matcher fence includes Site recompute-job statuses; report `FindingFenceMoved` as retryable typed S4 `FENCE_MOVED`, not a generic success. Preserve S3's five-minute lease and retry and S4-02c's Site claim/append idempotency. An expired/crashed claim resumes without duplicate output. Bound tick and Site wall time with server-side `statement_timeout` and `lock_timeout`, plus a killable child process per stage for a Python hang. The Site S3 stage bound must be shorter than the five-minute lease; after a killed job, expose its lease expiry and retry, never assert immediate completion. Both DB stall and nonreturning-stage injections must yield typed `TIMEOUT` and retryable work. Give schedule-level discovery/stage failures retry/backoff without advancing success; do not add an independent retry to S3 or consequence rows. A terminal FAILED S3 job stays live and will not be re-enqueued by `reconcile`; expose its ID and `last_error`, alarm, and provide `assess_dispatch requeue --tenant-id --site-id --start --end` through the existing `AssessmentStore.enqueue` path for an operator to re-enqueue that default window after fixing the cause. Document the command and confirm the new DEFAULT selection supersedes the old failure; `assess` creates an EXPLICIT run and is not recovery.
-3. **Durable state and health.** Migration `0013` adds `schedule_tick_head` (mutable environment owner, last tick start/success/failure, heartbeat, retry due and claim age), `schedule_site_head` (mutable per-Site claim/stage, selected/processed/deferred counts and last failure), `schedule_cursor` (mutable fair position), and `schedule_enrolment` (mutable explicit Site enrolment). Use DB claims or equivalent expiring ownership in these records; do not add unbounded tick rows by default. A new `backend/assetops_backend/scheduling/adapters/postgres_schedule_store.py` is the sole writer for these tables. Amend `tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1` rule 8 to name this eighth writer, its exact tables and allowed updates, with a reviewed S4-02d guard comment; this guard change is in scope and awaits Architect ratification, not pre-approved. Keep durable heads bounded; if attempt history is added, set a retention policy while preserving the latest failure and aggregate counts. Derive S3 backlog from live jobs, S4 backlog from existing `PostgresFindingStore.match_state` / `finding_match_pass` versus `analytic_run_selection.seq`, and consequence backlog from `ConsequenceStore.pending_work` with attempt count/age. Also count pre-0012 latest consequence heads whose read says `STALE` plus `NO_WORK_RECORDED` as distinct `STALE_NO_WORK` entries with Finding/revision IDs. This slice reports those discrepancies and blocks CURRENT; it does not backfill them. A read-only operator report gives each Site and aggregate `RUNNING`, `BACKLOG`, `RETRY_DUE`, `FAILED`, `STOPPED` or healthy idle. Only a completed zero-work tick advances heartbeat; claimed/skipped, failed and timed-out ticks do not. Repeated Site-claim skips, stale in-flight claims, old failures and growing uncapped consequence attempts trigger alarms and documented recovery.

+### Implementer carries from fresh re-review
+
+- **N2 — timeout propagation.** S3, Finding and consequence stores each construct an engine. Apply `statement_timeout` and `lock_timeout` to each stage child through its connection settings or schedule credential without moving engine construction into the orchestrator or editing another writer. Database access by the orchestrator and S5 seam remains through writer/store modules; rule 8 still rejects new SQL or driver imports outside the allowed writers.
+- **N3 — deterministic matcher failure.** `FindingFenceMoved` can also mean a cited evidence ID is not accepted in this Site. Distinguish that deterministic defect from a moved fence, or alarm on repeated `FENCE_MOVED` for the same Site so it cannot retry invisibly forever.
+- **N4 — registered evidence.** Define the active-Site predicate's “registered evidence” as at least one `accepted_evidence` row, or explicitly include registered gateway/source state. Prove an imported Site with neither is excluded unless enrolled and a Site with a gateway but no evidence follows the chosen definition.
+- **N5 — failed-job requeue.** `assess_dispatch requeue` must overlap the FAILED job's window for the new DEFAULT selection to supersede that failure; prefer `--job-id` or validate `--start/--end`. Preserve UUID tenant handling, avoid a demo-only restriction, and invoke the scheduled consequence stage by IDs rather than its one-off CLI.
+- **N6 — tick-only walkthrough.** Grep the full command log for `recompute_consequences run` as well as `process-jobs` and `match-findings`. Set per-tick limits so KOBO-001 completes S3 and S4 on the first post-release tick. If S3 times out, report the actual same-revision or deferred state instead of asserting the r2 path.

+### Implementer carries from fresh re-review
+
+- **N2 — timeout propagation.** S3, Finding and consequence stores each construct an engine. Apply `statement_timeout` and `lock_timeout` to each stage child through its connection settings or schedule credential without moving engine construction into the orchestrator or editing another writer. Database access by the orchestrator and S5 seam remains through writer/store modules; rule 8 still rejects new SQL or driver imports outside the allowed writers.
+- **N3 — deterministic matcher failure.** `FindingFenceMoved` can also mean a cited evidence ID is not accepted in this Site. Distinguish that deterministic defect from a moved fence, or alarm on repeated `FENCE_MOVED` for the same Site so it cannot retry invisibly forever.
+- **N4 — registered evidence.** Define the active-Site predicate's “registered evidence” as at least one `accepted_evidence` row, or explicitly include registered gateway/source state. Prove an imported Site with neither is excluded unless enrolled and a Site with a gateway but no evidence follows the chosen definition.
+- **N5 — failed-job requeue.** `assess_dispatch requeue` must overlap the FAILED job's window for the new DEFAULT selection to supersede that failure; prefer `--job-id` or validate `--start/--end`. Preserve UUID tenant handling, avoid a demo-only restriction, and invoke the scheduled consequence stage by IDs rather than its one-off CLI.
+- **N6 — tick-only walkthrough.** Grep the full command log for `recompute_consequences run` as well as `process-jobs` and `match-findings`. Set per-tick limits so KOBO-001 completes S3 and S4 on the first post-release tick. If S3 times out, report the actual same-revision or deferred state instead of asserting the r2 path.
